7079// #======================================================================
7080/// Named scope bracketing a subfile LaTeXML included itself — a `standalone`
7081/// child's preamble, an `\import`ed file. Real LaTeX has no group at either spot
7082/// (standalone *gobbles* the child preamble; import restores its paths by plain
7083/// `\def` after the `\input`), so a package loaded inside one is an artifact of
7084/// LaTeXML executing what the real packages skip. Bindings that open such a
7085/// bracket activate this scope; `require_package` reads it to decide whether a
7086/// load must outlive the bracket. See OXIDIZED_DESIGN #65.
7087///
7088/// The name carries the frame depth of the bracket that opened it — Perl's own
7089/// `section:4` / `label:foo` convention (State.pm L965-975) — because activity
7090/// alone is not enough: `StashActive` is `Scope::Local` at the bracket's frame,
7091/// so a plain "is the region active?" test is ALSO true at every deeper frame,
7092/// and an author's `{\usepackage{…}}` written *inside* a subfile preamble would
7093/// be hoisted as well. That is a downgrade: pdflatex and Perl both leave such a
7094/// package lost. Matching the depth confines the region to the bracket's own
7095/// level.
7096pub fn subfile_scope_at_depth(depth: usize) -> SymStr { arena::pin(format!("subfile:{depth}")) }
7097
7098/// The subfile scope for the CURRENT frame depth — what a bracket activates on
7099/// opening, and what `require_package` tests before hoisting.
7100pub fn subfile_scope_here() -> SymStr { subfile_scope_at_depth(get_frame_depth()) }
7101
7102/// Is the named scope currently active? See `scope_active_in` for why this is a
7103/// front-value test and not a presence test, and `subfile_scope_at_depth` for the region
7104/// marker it supports.
7105pub fn is_scope_active(scope: SymStr) -> bool { scope_active_in(&state!(), scope) }
7106
7107/// Perl's scope-activity test, shared by the three call sites that need it
7108/// (`is_scope_active`, `activate_scope`, `deactivate_scope`; `get_active_scopes`
7109/// deliberately still enumerates KEYS, faithful to Perl State.pm L722-725, which
7110/// has the same latent quirk — it has no callers):
7111/// the truthiness of the FRONT `stash_active` value — `$$self{stash_active}
7112/// {$scope}[0]` in `activateScope` (State.pm L682) and `deactivateScope`
7113/// (L700).
7114///
7115/// Presence is NOT the test, and cannot be: deactivation OVERWRITES the front
7116/// value with a falsy one rather than removing the key — an ordinary global
7117/// assignment (`assign_internal(… 'stash_active', $scope, 0, 'global')`,
7118/// State.pm L701; ours passes `Stored::Bool(false)`). A global assign replaces
7119/// rather than layers: it drops the per-frame counts down to the locked frame,
7120/// pops exactly that many values, and leaves ONE. So the front value is the
7121/// whole state. (A delete is not available anyway — `stash_active` rides the
7122/// generic table + undo machinery, whose per-frame pop counts a removed key
7123/// would desynchronise.) Pinned by
7124/// `reentrancy_tests::scope_activity_tracks_value_not_presence`.
7125///
7126/// Reach: the production consumers — `counter/dialect.rs` (a reference number's
7127/// `<ctr>:<refnum>` scope, deactivated then re-activated as the counter moves,
7128/// mirroring Perl `Package.pm` L774-779) and `latex_constructs.rs`'s `label:`
7129/// scopes — activate names that nothing currently STASHES into, so an activation
7130/// installs no bindings and the re-activation fix is correct but latent. It
7131/// becomes observable the moment a binding defines with `scope => "<ctr>:<n>"`.
7132/// That is why the guards are unit-level: there is no output difference to
7133/// assert end-to-end yet. (The one `Scope::Named` stash writer, `declare.rs`'s
7134/// declaration scope — `id:<unit id>` or `label:<label>` — is consumed by `rewrite.rs`
7135/// by prefix, not by activation.)
7136///
7137/// The local/global asymmetry is deliberate (Perl's own note above
7138/// `deactivateScope`): activation is `local`, so it expires with its group
7139/// without a teardown call — which is what makes a named scope usable as a
7140/// region marker (see `subfile_scope_at_depth`) — while deactivation is `global` so it
7141/// survives group exit. A local deactivation would be undone by the very group
7142/// that contained it.
7143fn scope_active_in(state: &State, scope: SymStr) -> bool {
7144  state
7145    .stash_active
7146    .get(&scope)
7147    .and_then(|entry| entry.front())
7148    .is_some_and(|v| !matches!(v, Stored::Bool(false)))
7149}
7150
7151/// Activates all stashed definitions for the named scope. No-op if the scope is already active.
7152pub fn activate_scope(scope: SymStr) {
7153  let mut state = state_mut!();
7154  // Perl L682 `if (!$$self{stash_active}{$scope}[0])` — do not re-activate if
7155  // already active, but a scope that was DEACTIVATED must be activatable again.
7156  if scope_active_in(&state, scope) {
7157    return;
7158  }
7159
7160  state.assign_internal(
7161    TableName::StashActive,
7162    scope,
7163    Stored::Bool(true),
7164    Some(Scope::Local),
7165  );
7166  // Also, we need to take ownership of the stashed data, so that we can assign it.
7167  // TODO: Potential to optimize?
7168  // Also x2, we are using a shared "Stored" interface for all data that passes through
7169  // assign_internal, but that causes both uncertainty and overhead in the Stash table
7170  // specifically. TODO x2: Maybe a more ambitious refactor will separate out the Stash logic
7171  // and use "StashTable" directly instead of Stored::Stash(StashTable) ?
7172
7173  let mut actions = Vec::new();
7174
7175  if let Some(Some(Stored::Stash(defns))) = state.stash.get(&scope).map(|x| x.iter().next()) {
7176    for (table_name, key, value) in defns {
7177      // copy the values out from the stashed defns, so that Rust
7178      // is calm we are borrowing safely.
7179
7180      actions.push((*table_name, key.to_owned(), value.clone()));
7181    }
7182  }
7183  // Here we ALWAYS push the stashed values into the table
7184  // since they may be popped off by deactivateScope
7185  for (table_name, key, value) in actions {
7186    state.journal_touch(table_name, key);
7187    let frame = &mut state.undo[0];
7188    let frame_table = frame.table_mut(table_name);
7189    let entry = frame_table.entry(key).or_insert(0);
7190    *entry += 1; // Note that this many values must be undone
7191    let key_table = state.table_mut(table_name).entry(key).or_default();
7192    key_table.push_front(value); // And push new binding.
7193  }
7194}
7195
7196// Probably, in most cases, the assignments made by activateScope
7197// will be undone by egroup or popping frames.
7198// But they can also be undone explicitly
7199
7200/// Removes any definitions that were associated with the named `scope`.
7201/// Normally not needed, since a scopes definitions are locally bound anyway.
7202pub fn deactivate_scope(scope: SymStr) {
7203  let mut state = state_mut!();
7204  // Perl L700 `if ($$self{stash_active}{$scope}[0])` — only an ACTIVE scope is
7205  // deactivated; a second deactivation must not re-run the pop below.
7206  if !scope_active_in(&state, scope) {
7207    return;
7208  }
7209
7210  state.assign_internal(
7211    TableName::StashActive,
7212    scope,
7213    Stored::Bool(false),
7214    Some(Scope::Global),
7215  );
7216
7217  let mut collected = Vec::new();
7218  if let Some(Some(Stored::Stash(defns))) = state.stash.get(&scope).map(|x| x.iter().next()) {
7219    for (table_name, key, value) in defns {
7220      collected.push((table_name.to_owned(), key.to_owned(), value.to_owned()));
7221    }
7222  }
7223
7224  for (table_name, key, value) in collected {
7225    state.journal_touch(table_name, key);
7226    let front_is_value = if let Some(table_entry_peek) = state.table(table_name).get(&key) {
7227      if let Some(table_front) = table_entry_peek.front() {
7228        *table_front == value
7229      } else {
7230        false
7231      }
7232    } else {
7233      false
7234    };
7235    let table_entry = state.table_mut(table_name).entry(key).or_default();
7236    if front_is_value {
7237      // Here we're popping off the values pushed by activateScope
7238      // to (possibly) reveal a local assignment in the same frame, preceding activateScope.
7239      (*table_entry).pop_front();
7240
7241      if let Some(frame) = state.undo.front_mut() {
7242        let frame_table = frame.table_mut(table_name);
7243        let frame_count = frame_table.entry(key).or_default();
7244        *frame_count -= 1;
7245      }
7246    } else {
7247      let message = arena::with(key, |key_str| {
7248        s!(
7249          "Unassigning wrong value for {} from table {} in deactivateScopevalue is {:?} but stack \
7250          is {:?}",
7251          key_str,
7252          table_name,
7253          value,
7254          table_entry
7255            .iter()
7256            .map(ToString::to_string)
7257            .collect::<Vec<String>>()
7258            .join(", ")
7259        )
7260      });
7261      arena::with(key, |key_str| Warn!("internal", key_str, message));
7262    }
7263  }
7264}
7265/// return all known named scopes
7266pub fn get_known_scopes() -> Vec<SymStr> { state!().stash.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>() }
7267/// return the currently activated named scopes
7268pub fn get_active_scopes() -> Vec<SymStr> {
7269  state!().stash_active.keys().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>()
7270}
7271
7272//======================================================================
7273// Units.
7274// Put here since it could concievably evolve to depend on the current font.
7275/// convert a unit name into a `f64` scaling factor over `sp`
7276pub fn convert_unit(unit_arg: &str) -> f64 {
7277  let unit = unit_arg.to_lowercase();
7278  // Font-relative units fall back to 10pt metrics when no current font is
7279  // set (e.g. pre-bootstrap unit conversion). Perl gets this via the
7280  // built-in default font; matching with a static fallback is cheaper
7281  // than forcing every caller to ensure a font frame exists.
7282  let font_metric =
7283    |getter: fn(&Font) -> i64| -> f64 { lookup_font().map(|f| getter(&f) as f64).unwrap_or(0.0) };
7284  match unit.as_str() {
7285    "em" | "zw" | "zh" => font_metric(|f| f.get_em_width()),
7286    "ex" => font_metric(|f| f.get_ex_height()),
7287    "mu" => font_metric(|f| f.get_mu_width()),
7288    u => match UNITS.get(u) {
7289      Some(sp) => *sp,
7290      None => {
7291        let message = s!("Illegal unit of measure {:?}, assuming pt.", u);
7292        Warn!("expected", "<unit>", message);
7293        *UNITS.get("pt").unwrap()
7294      },
7295    },
7296  }
7297}
7298
7299/// Convert a unit name into the exact TeX `(num, den)` fraction such that a
7300/// dimension of `value` units is `floor(round(value·65536)·num/den)` scaled
7301/// points (see `numeric_ops::fixpoint_unit`).
7302///
7303/// Physical units use TeX's `set_conversion(num)(denom)` fractions verbatim
7304/// (tex.web §458, lines 9020-9032): `in=7227/100, pc=12/1, cm=7227/254,
7305/// mm=7227/2540, bp=7227/7200, dd=1238/1157, cc=14856/1157`, plus `pt=1/1` and
7306/// `sp=1/65536`. `px` follows LaTeXML in aliasing `bp`. Font-relative units
7307/// (`em`/`ex`/`mu`) return `(metric_sp, 65536)`, matching tex.web §8983's
7308/// `nx_plus_y(_, v, xn_over_d(v, f, 65536))` for internal units. This is the
7309/// exact-integer counterpart of [`convert_unit`]; each physical entry satisfies
7310/// `convert_unit(u) == 65536·num/den`.
7311pub fn convert_unit_ratio(unit_arg: &str) -> (i64, i64) {
7312  let unit = unit_arg.to_lowercase();
7313  let font_metric =
7314    |getter: fn(&Font) -> i64| -> i64 { lookup_font().map(|f| getter(&f)).unwrap_or(0) };
7315  // UNITY == 65536 sp per pt; font-relative and `sp` units convert via the
7316  // `floor(fix·v/UNITY)` path (tex.web §8983 nx_plus_y/xn_over_d).
7317  match unit.as_str() {
7318    "em" | "zw" | "zh" => (font_metric(|f| f.get_em_width()), UNITY),
7319    "ex" => (font_metric(|f| f.get_ex_height()), UNITY),
7320    "mu" => (font_metric(|f| f.get_mu_width()), UNITY),
7321    "pt" => (1, 1),
7322    "pc" => (12, 1),
7323    "in" => (7227, 100),
7324    "bp" | "px" => (7227, 7200),
7325    "cm" => (7227, 254),
7326    "mm" => (7227, 2540),
7327    "dd" => (1238, 1157),
7328    "cc" => (14856, 1157),
7329    "sp" => (1, UNITY),
7330    u => {
7331      let message = s!("Illegal unit of measure {:?}, assuming pt.", u);
7332      Warn!("expected", "<unit>", message);
7333      (1, 1)
7334    },
7335  }
7336}

7380// TODO: Continue here -- need to diagnose why the indirect model is not returning
7381// an intermediate "ltx:p" when asking for "#PCDATA" inside "ltx:_CaptureBlock_",
7382// instead getting an intermediate "ltx:para".
7383
7384/// The indirect model includes all elements allowed as direct children,
7385/// and all descendents of a node that can be inserted after autoOpen'ing intermediate elements.
7386///
7387/// This model therefor includes information from the Schema, as well as
7388/// `auto_open` information that may be introduced in binding files.
7389// [Thus it should NOT be modifying the Model object, which may cover several documents in Daemon]
7390// `imodel[tag][child] => inter` means if in `tag`, to open `child`, we must first open `inter`
7391pub fn compute_indirect_model() -> IndirectModel {
7392  let mut imodel: IndirectModel = SymHashMap::default();
7393  // Determine any indirect paths to each descendent via an `autoOpen-able' tag.
7394  // Perl Document.pm L196-199 maps the `autoOpen` property to a fractional
7395  // OPENABILITY. Most tags get 1.0; `ltx:picture` gets 0.5 (latex_constructs.pool.ltxml:4943) so it
7396  // loses path-priority against full auto-openers (para, p, text, item, …).
7397  // We scale to u32 (100 = full, 50 = half, `AutoOpen`) to keep integer
7398  // arithmetic; the `desirability * openability / 100` recursion mirrors
7399  // Perl's float math. An openability of 0 is no auto-opener (Perl's falsy
7400  // `$::OPENABILITY{$kid}`, L216).
7401  let mut openability: SymHashMap<u32> = SymHashMap::default();
7402  // Collect all known tags: from the schema model AND from state tag_properties
7403  let mut all_tags: HashSet<SymStr> = model::get_tags().into_iter().collect();
7404  for tag in state!().tag_properties.keys() {
7405    all_tags.insert(*tag);
7406  }
7407  for tag in &all_tags {
7408    if let Some(x) = state!().tag_properties.get(tag)
7409      && let Some(AutoOpen(priority)) = x.auto_open
7410      && priority > 0
7411    {
7412      openability.insert_sym(*tag, priority);
7413    }
7414  }
7415
7416  for tag in &all_tags {
7417    let tag = *tag;
7418    let mut desc: SymHashMap<SymHashMap<usize>> = SymHashMap::default();
7419    compute_indirect_model_aux(tag, None, 100, &mut openability, &mut desc);
7420    let desc_keys: Vec<SymStr> = desc.keys().copied().collect();
7421    for kid in desc_keys {
7422      // Find best path to `kid`.
7423      let mut best = 0;
7424      let mut desc_kid_keys: Vec<SymStr> =
7425        desc.entry_sym(kid).or_default().keys().copied().collect();
7426      // TODO: why sort?
7427      // Update: it appears that "ltx:p" and "ltx:para" in ltx:_CaptureBlock_ is one reason!!!
7428      desc_kid_keys.sort_by(|a, b| arena::with2(*a, *b, |astr, bstr| astr.cmp(bstr)));
7429      for start in desc_kid_keys {
7430        if tag != kid && tag != start {
7431          let start_entry = {
7432            let kid_entry = desc.entry_sym(kid).or_default();
7433            *kid_entry.entry_sym(start).or_insert(0)
7434          };
7435          if start_entry > best {
7436            imodel.entry_sym(tag).or_default().insert_sym(kid, start);
7437            {
7438              best = start_entry;
7439            }
7440          }
7441        }
7442      }
7443    }
7444  }
7445  // PATCHUP
7446  if model::is_permissive() {
7447    // !!! Alarm!!!
7448    imodel
7449      .entry("#Document")
7450      .or_default()
7451      .insert("#PCDATA", arena::pin_static("ltx:p"));
7452  }
7453
7454  imodel
7455}
7456
7457// Package helpers used in core need to be localized here -- as state methods
7458/// `Let` macro setter
7459/// Run `body` with `cs` temporarily `\let` to `target` (local scope), then
7460/// restore `cs`'s prior meaning — the `{\let\cs\target …}` idiom of latex.ltx
7461/// (`\protected@edef`'s `\let\protect\@unexpandable@protect`, :1442) without
7462/// a save-stack group, for a reader that must not open one. The prior
7463/// meaning is restored whether or not `body` fails. A `cs` with no meaning
7464/// is restored to none.
7465pub fn with_let<R>(cs: &Token, target: &Token, body: impl FnOnce() -> Result<R>) -> Result<R> {
7466  let saved = lookup_meaning(cs);
7467  let_i(cs, target, None);
7468  let result = body();
7469  match saved {
7470    Some(meaning) => assign_meaning(cs, meaning, None),
7471    None => assign_meaning(cs, Stored::None, None),
7472  }
7473  result
7474}
7475
7476/// A binding's `\let` (`Let!`): [`let_i`], unless a provide-only load declines it ([`provide_only_declines`]).
7477/// TeX's own `\let` and `\futurelet` are `let_i`.
7478pub fn let_definition(token1: &Token, token2: &Token, scope: Option<Scope>) {
7479  if !provide_only_declines(token1) {
7480    let_i(token1, token2, scope);
7481  }
7482}
7483
7484pub fn let_i(token1: &Token, token2: &Token, scope: Option<Scope>) {
7485  {
7486    use crate::definition::origin::{DefinitionOrigin, current_origin, stand_in_loading};
7487    // (only while a stand-in binding loads, whose `\let`s a copy may meet: not every `\newif` flag of the pool)
7488    if stand_in_loading()
7489      && matches!(
7490        current_origin(),
7491        DefinitionOrigin::Unknown | DefinitionOrigin::Pool | DefinitionOrigin::Binding
7492      )
7493      && matches!(token1.get_catcode(), Catcode::CS | Catcode::ACTIVE)
7494    {
7495      let key = meaning_key(token1);
7496      state_mut!().binding_lets.insert(key);
7497    }
7498  }
7499  let meaning =// if token2.get_dont_expand().is_some() {
7500  //   Stored::Token(token2.clone())
7501  // } else {
7502    lookup_meaning(token2)
7503      .unwrap_or(Stored::None);
7504  // };
7505  if copy_provides_keeps_saved_meaning(token1, token2, &meaning) {
7506    after_assignment();
7507    return;
7508  }
7509  // A lock against the class and package files (`"{cs}:locked@files"`, [`name_carries_lock`]) holds for their `\let`
7510  // as for their `\def` ([`install_definition`]): arabtex's asect.sty:297 `\let\endabstract\endquotation`, which its
7511  // own raw `\end` (alatex.sty:105-109) then calls, closed the kernel abstract's body as a quotation (2401.14559). A
7512  // plain `:locked` is left to a `\let` to a non-robust meaning, as Perl's `Let` checks none (Package.pm:383-390); a
7513  // `\let` to a robust command takes the body copy below, whose wrapper the lock refuses (OXIDIZED_DESIGN #568). So
7514  // are the engine's own meaning writes ([`assign_meaning`]: `\csname`'s `\relax`, restores). A `\let` to an undefined token only clears
7515  // the name, and is kept: the "undefine, then define anew" idiom (projlib-author.sty:243-246 `\cs_undefine:N
7516  // \endabstract` before `\newenvsc{abstract}`, whose scontents `\NewDocumentEnvironment` "already defined" check
7517  // would fail; the homework and jwjournal manuals). The binding is kept aside (`"{cs}:locked@cleared"`) and back in
7518  // place when the file's new meaning is refused ([`restore_cleared_binding`]), whether the name is still undefined or
7519  // `\csname` has made it `\relax` on the way. Read at the origins `install_definition` reads the lock at (every one
7520  // but the document's, [`name_carries_lock`]). OXIDIZED_DESIGN #569; guards `perfect_kernel_batch66b::file_let_*`,
7521  // `…::refused_*_restores_the_binding`.
7522  // A lock that holds only against copies of the binding's interface ([`files_lock_holds`]) judges the `\let` by the
7523  // meaning it copies: one taking an optional argument with a default first is refused as above; any other goes
7524  // through, the binding kept aside as for a clearing `\let`, so that a copy defined next and refused puts it back
7525  // (a package's `\let\proof\relax` … `\newenvironment{proof}[1][\proofname]`; guard
7526  // `perfect_kernel_batch67s::package_redefined_proof_keeps_the_binding`). Not GT.cls (2503.05183): its `\let\proof
7527  // \relax` (:1469-1470) is the class's, but its copy is defined inside `\AtBeginDocument` (:1488-1494), at the
7528  // document's origin, and honoured (OXIDIZED_DESIGN_DIVERGENCES #596).
7529  if crate::definition::origin::current_origin()
7530    != crate::definition::origin::DefinitionOrigin::Document
7531    && !state_is_unlocked()
7532    && matches!(token1.get_catcode(), Catcode::CS | Catcode::ACTIVE)
7533    && token1.with_cs_name(|cs| arena::get(s!("{cs}:locked@files")).is_some_and(lookup_bool_sym))
7534  {
7535    let cs = token1.with_cs_name(|cs| cs.to_string());
7536    let holds = files_lock_holds(&cs, || takes_optional_first(&meaning));
7537    if holds && !matches!(meaning, Stored::None) {
7538      restore_cleared_binding(token1);
7539      after_assignment();
7540      return;
7541    }
7542    let protected = copies_lock(&cs);
7543    let clearing = matches!(meaning, Stored::None) || x_equals(token2, &crate::T_CS!("\\relax"));
7544    if clearing {
7545      if !is_cleared_meaning(token1)
7546        && protected
7547          .as_ref()
7548          .is_none_or(|protected| copies_lock_in_force(&cs, protected))
7549        && let Some(binding) = lookup_meaning(token1)
7550      {
7551        let cleared_key = token1.with_cs_name(|cs| s!("{cs}:locked@cleared"));
7552        assign_value(&cleared_key, binding, Some(Scope::Global));
7553      }
7554    } else {
7555      drop_copies_lock_aside(&cs);
7556    }
7557  } else if !state_is_unlocked()
7558    && matches!(token1.get_catcode(), Catcode::CS | Catcode::ACTIVE)
7559    && (matches!(meaning, Stored::None) || x_equals(token2, &crate::T_CS!("\\relax")))
7560    && !is_cleared_meaning(token1)
7561    && token1.with_cs_name(|cs| arena::get(s!("{cs}:locked")).is_some_and(lookup_bool_sym))
7562    && let Some(binding) = lookup_meaning(token1)
7563  {
7564    // A plain `:locked` binding a file's `\let` clears is kept aside too, the `\let` itself going through as in Perl,
7565    // and back in place when the file's own new meaning is refused ([`restore_cleared_binding`]): eulerpx.sty:130-132
7566    // `\let\mathscr=\undefined` then `\DeclareMathAlphabet\mathscr{U}{zeus}{m}{n}`, which the lock on the kernel's
7567    // `\mathscr` (sect07.rs) refuses, left `\mathscr` undefined, an Error at every use (2311.14793, 2501.10505,
7568    // 2510.26876; KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS #863). OXIDIZED_DESIGN #569. The document's own `\let` too, at every origin: the
7569    // plain lock refuses the document's redefinition as it refuses a file's (`\let\mathcal\undefined
7570    // \DeclareMathAlphabet{\mathcal}{OMS}{cmsy}{m}{n}` left `\mathcal` undefined; `\let\mathscr\relax` before
7571    // `\DeclareMathAlphabet{\mathscr}{U}{rsfs}{m}{n}` lost its script font), and a stale binding a package's
7572    // group-local clear set aside is replaced by the current one. A document value-definition after the restore still
7573    // reaches the binding's call owner (OXIDIZED_DESIGN #500). The `:locked@files` branch above keeps its origin test.
7574    let cleared_key = token1.with_cs_name(|cs| s!("{cs}:locked@cleared"));
7575    assign_value(&cleared_key, binding, Some(Scope::Global));
7576  }
7577  // Deep-copy the robust-wrapper pair.
7578  //
7579  // Our `DefConstructor`/`DefMacro` with `robust => true` stores the
7580  // public CS (e.g. `\ref`) as an Expandable wrapper that expands to
7581  // `\protect \<cs><space>`. The actual body lives under a SEPARATE
7582  // `\<cs><space>` slot. A plain `\let \origref \ref` would copy
7583  // only the wrapper — leaving the `\ref<space>` body shared between
7584  // `\origref` and `\ref`. A subsequent `\DeclareRobustCommand \ref
7585  // {...}` then overwrites `\ref<space>` and `\origref` silently
7586  // tracks the new body — often causing an infinite loop when the
7587  // new body references `\origref` itself (a common LaTeX idiom for
7588  // adding starred-form support: `\let\origref\ref
7589  // \DeclareRobustCommand\ref{\@ifstar\origref\origref}`).
7590  //
7591  // So the body half is `\let` too: `\let \origref<space> \ref<space>`, and
7592  // the two CSes own independent body slots. This is NOT TeX's `\let`, which
7593  // copies the wrapper `\protect \ref<space>` itself (and loops on that idiom
7594  // for a robust `\ref`; latex.ltx's `\ref` is not robust, the binding's is):
7595  // `\meaning\origref` reads `\protect \origref  ` where TeX reads `\protect
7596  // \ref  `, and `\ifx` compares the bodies ([`same_robust_body`]).
7597  // OXIDIZED_DESIGN_DIVERGENCES #315.
7598  //
7599  // Witnesses: canvas-3 stage-23 0810.0695 (PlanarMain.tex's
7600  // `\ifpdf...\else \let\origref\ref \DeclareRobustCommand\ref{
7601  // \@ifstar\origref\origref}\fi` triggers via the else-branch
7602  // when `\ifpdf` is false; its `\pdfoutput=1` makes it true since K6).
7603  //
7604  // Not when the body calls `\<token1><space>` itself: copied there it would call itself, so `\<token1><space>` is left
7605  // alone, as TeX's `\let` leaves it, and `\<token1>` takes `\<token2>`'s wrapper. mathfixs' autobold
7606  // (mathfixs.sty:24-32) builds `\mafx@bfseries` by one-step expansion of the robust `\bfseries` — its body
7607  // `\protect\bfseries<space>\ifmmode…` — and then `\let\bfseries=\mafx@bfseries` (33 of run 336's papers ran to
7608  // PushbackLimit, 2205.10090, 2211.07279). A copy over an existing body slot is still a copy (`\DeclareCommandCopy`
7609  // onto a robust command, `\let\origref\ref` run twice).
7610  //
7611  // Not over a locked inner: the body copy writes `\<token1><space>` directly, so for a locked robust binding (the
7612  // kernel `\cite`, whose inner is locked against the files, OXIDIZED_DESIGN #568) a class's `\let\cite\mycite` would
7613  // put its body under the binding's own wrapper, whose reinstallation the lock then refuses. Such a `\let` takes
7614  // TeX's (and Perl's `Let`) plain path below instead. A locked binding whose inner is free (a constructor: the
7615  // kernel `\LaTeX`) keeps the copy, whose wrapper the lock refuses: bera.sty:12-23's `\let\LaTeX=\fveLaTeX` (a
7616  // `\DeclareRobustCommand`) would otherwise replace the logo with its typeset boxes (enumitem, colorspace and mathador
7617  // manuals); its `\let\TeX=\fveTeX` (:10-11, a `\def`) takes the plain path and replaces `\TeX`, as in Perl. Guard
7618  // `perfect_kernel_batch66b::robust_let_over_a_locked_constructor_keeps_it`.
7619  if let Stored::Expandable(ref defn) = meaning
7620    && let Some(token2_space) = robust_wrapper_body(defn, token2)
7621    && !token1.with_str(|s| body_mentions(&token2_space, &s!("{s} ")))
7622    && !token1.with_str(|s| is_name_locked(&s!("{s} ")))
7623  {
7624    let token1_space = crate::T_CS!(token1.with_str(|s| s!("{s} ")));
7625    // (1) Copy `\<token2><space>` body to `\<token1><space>`
7626    // so the two CSes have independent body slots.
7627    let body_meaning = lookup_meaning(&token2_space).unwrap_or(Stored::None);
7628    let body_csname_sym = token1_space.pin_cs_name();
7629    state_mut!().assign_internal(TableName::Meaning, body_csname_sym, body_meaning, scope);
7630    // (2) Install `\<token1>` as a NEW robust wrapper that
7631    // points to `\<token1><space>` (rather than reusing
7632    // `\<token2>`'s wrapper, which still hardcodes
7633    // `\<token2><space>` in its body and would silently
7634    // re-track any later `\DeclareRobustCommand\<token2>{...}`).
7635    let new_wrapper_body = Tokens::new(vec![crate::T_CS!("\\protect"), token1_space]);
7636    let new_wrapper = Expandable::new(
7637      *token1,
7638      None,
7639      Some(ExpansionBody::Tokens(new_wrapper_body)),
7640      Some(expandable::ExpandableOptions {
7641        robust: true,
7642        ..expandable::ExpandableOptions::default()
7643      }),
7644    );
7645    if let Ok(wrapper) = new_wrapper {
7646      install_definition(wrapper, scope);
7647      after_assignment();
7648      return;
7649    }
7650  }
7651  assign_meaning(token1, meaning, scope);
7652  after_assignment();
7653}
7654/// `XEquals` check for two token arguments
7655pub fn x_equals(token1: &Token, token2: &Token) -> bool {
7656  let def1_opt = lookup_meaning(token1); // # token, definition object or None
7657  let def2_opt = lookup_meaning(token2); // ditto
7658  match (def1_opt, def2_opt) {
7659    (Some(def1), Some(def2)) => def1 == def2 || same_robust_body(token1, &def1, token2, &def2),
7660    (None, None) => true, // true if both undefined
7661    (..) => false,        // False, if only one has 'meaning'
7662  }
7663}
7664
7665/// Whether the macro `cs`'s token body mentions the control sequence named `name` (a closure body is opaque: no). By
7666/// name, so no token is interned for a name that may never exist.
7667fn body_mentions(cs: &Token, name: &str) -> bool {
7668  match lookup_meaning(cs) {
7669    Some(Stored::Expandable(defn)) => match defn.expansion {
7670      Some(ExpansionBody::Tokens(ref tks)) => {
7671        tks.unlist_ref().iter().any(|t| t.with_str(|s| s == name))
7672      },
7673      _ => false,
7674    },
7675    _ => false,
7676  }
7677}
7678
7679/// The inner control sequence of a robust wrapper: `defn` is `cs`'s meaning,
7680/// a macro whose expansion is exactly `\protect \<cs><space>`
7681/// (`\DeclareRobustCommand`, a binding's `robust => true`, or [`let_i`]'s copy
7682/// of either).
7683fn robust_wrapper_body(defn: &Expandable, cs: &Token) -> Option<Token> {
7684  let Some(ExpansionBody::Tokens(ref tks)) = defn.expansion else {
7685    return None;
7686  };
7687  let body = tks.unlist_ref();
7688  if body.len() != 2 || !body[0].with_str(|s| s == "\\protect") {
7689    return None;
7690  }
7691  let inner = cs.with_str(|s| s!("{s} "));
7692  body[1]
7693    .with_str(|s| s == inner)
7694    .then(|| crate::T_CS!(inner))
7695}
7696
7697/// `\ifx` of two robust wrappers whose inner macros have the same meaning.
7698/// In TeX `\let\x\bfseries` copies the wrapper `\protect \bfseries␣` itself,
7699/// so `\ifx\x\bfseries` is true (tex.web §507 compares the token lists);
7700/// [`let_i`] instead gives `\x` its own wrapper `\protect \x␣` and a copy of
7701/// the body under `\x␣` (arXiv 0810.0695, OXIDIZED_DESIGN_DIVERGENCES #315), so
7702/// the wrappers differ by name only and the bodies decide. `\ifx\reset@font
7703/// \normalfont` relies on it, and in the preamble amsthm.sty:209-212's
7704/// `\nonslanted` (`\let\@tempa\csname\f@shape shape\endcsname \ifx\@tempa
7705/// \itshape`; in the body the shape switches are `\protected` macros, a plain
7706/// meaning comparison). The bodies must be the same definition, not only
7707/// equal: two robust commands declared apart with the same body compare
7708/// false, as in TeX. A `\LetLtxMacro` copy (letltxmacro.sty) and a
7709/// `\NewCommandCopy`/`\DeclareCommandCopy`/`\RenewCommandCopy` copy (each its
7710/// own wrapper over a `\let` of the body) compare true, where TeX says false:
7711/// their state is [`let_i`]'s.
7712fn same_robust_body(token1: &Token, def1: &Stored, token2: &Token, def2: &Stored) -> bool {
7713  let (Stored::Expandable(defn1), Stored::Expandable(defn2)) = (def1, def2) else {
7714    return false;
7715  };
7716  if defn1.paramlist != defn2.paramlist {
7717    return false;
7718  }
7719  match (
7720    robust_wrapper_body(defn1, token1),
7721    robust_wrapper_body(defn2, token2),
7722  ) {
7723    (Some(inner1), Some(inner2)) => match (lookup_meaning(&inner1), lookup_meaning(&inner2)) {
7724      // The body [`let_i`] copies is the source's own `Rc<Expandable>`, so its
7725      // `cs` still names the inner macro it was defined as (`\bfseries␣`, as
7726      // the dump keeps it): equal bodies from two separate declarations
7727      // (`\DeclareRobustCommand\ra{x}`, `\DeclareRobustCommand\rb{x}`) stay
7728      // unequal, as their wrappers are in TeX.
7729      (Some(Stored::Expandable(body1)), Some(Stored::Expandable(body2))) => {
7730        body1 == body2 && body1.cs == body2.cs
7731      },
7732      (Some(body1), Some(body2)) => body1 == body2,
7733      _ => false,
7734    },
7735    _ => false,
7736  }
7737}
7738
7739/// simple id generator for a ligature
7740pub fn generate_ligature_id() -> usize {
7741  let id = 1 + lookup_int("autogen_ligature_id");
7742  assign_value("autogen_ligature_id", Stored::Int(id), Scope::Global);
7743  id as usize
7744}
7745
7746/// run the accumulated directives from `\afterassignment`
7747pub fn after_assignment() {
7748  match remove_value_sym(pin!("afterAssignment")) {
7749    Some(Stored::Tokens(after)) => gullet::unread(after),
7750    Some(Stored::Token(after)) => gullet::unread_one(after),
7751    None | Some(Stored::None) => {},
7752    Some(other) => panic!("unexpected in after_assignment: {other:?}"),
7753  }
7754}

7792/// Find-or-append a source file in the document-level `sources` table,
7793/// returning its integer `tag` (index). The per-element `data-sourcepos`
7794/// attribute carries this compact integer rather than a path — the
7795/// Source-Map-v3 `sources` convention (compact + anonymisable). Only
7796/// called on the source-map path. See `docs/performance/SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` §0.1.
7797pub fn source_tag(source: SymStr) -> u32 {
7798  let mut state = state_mut!();
7799  if let Some(idx) = state.source_table.iter().position(|s| *s == source) {
7800    idx as u32
7801  } else {
7802    state.source_table.push(source);
7803    (state.source_table.len() - 1) as u32
7804  }
7805}

7808/// document-level tag→file header.
7809pub fn source_table_snapshot() -> Vec<SymStr> { state!().source_table.clone() }

7851  static PLAIN_TEX_DOCUMENT: std::cell::Cell<bool> = const { std::cell::Cell::new(false) };
7852}
7853/// Records whether the document about to be converted is a plain TeX one, by arXiv AutoTeX's test (its main file, or a
7854/// file a cue-less main file inputs, names neither `\documentclass`, `\documentstyle` nor `\begin{document}` before an
7855/// `\endinput`); set per conversion before the session's preloads load (`latexml::converter::Converter::note_main_source`).
7856pub fn set_plain_tex_document(plain: bool) { PLAIN_TEX_DOCUMENT.with(|c| c.set(plain)); }
7857/// Whether the document being converted is a plain TeX one ([`set_plain_tex_document`]): latexml.sty's preload must not
7858/// load the LaTeX format for it.
7859pub fn plain_tex_document() -> bool { PLAIN_TEX_DOCUMENT.with(std::cell::Cell::get) }
7860std::thread_local! {
7861  static DVI_DRIVER_OPTION: std::cell::Cell<bool> = const { std::cell::Cell::new(false) };
7862}
7863/// Records whether the main file's `\documentclass` names a DVI driver option (`dvips`, `dvipdfmx`, …) and the file sets
7864/// no `\pdfoutput=1`: the document is a latex+dvips one (the K6 ruling's DVI cue, stream F).
7865pub fn set_dvi_driver_option(dvi: bool) { DVI_DRIVER_OPTION.with(|c| c.set(dvi)); }

7868std::thread_local! {
7869  static PDF_DRIVER_OPTION: std::cell::Cell<bool> = const { std::cell::Cell::new(false) };
7870}
7871/// Records whether the main file's `\documentclass` names the `pdftex` driver option: the document is a pdflatex one,
7872/// whatever PostScript files it also ships (the K6 ruling's PDF cue, mirroring [`set_dvi_driver_option`]).
7873pub fn set_pdf_driver_option(pdf: bool) { PDF_DRIVER_OPTION.with(|c| c.set(pdf)); }

7875pub fn pdf_driver_option() -> bool { PDF_DRIVER_OPTION.with(std::cell::Cell::get) }
7876/// What the figures a source includes say of its output mode (the K6 ruling's figure cue,
7877/// OXIDIZED_DESIGN_DIVERGENCES #537): read from the `\includegraphics`-family targets the main file and the files it
7878/// inputs name, resolved against the source tree, and from a pstricks load.
7879#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
7880pub enum FigureOutputCue {
7881  /// No included figure resolves and no pstricks is loaded: the source tree's files decide.
7882  #[default]
7883  Undecided,
7884  /// Every included figure that resolves has a PostScript form, one has no other, or pstricks is loaded: latex+dvips.
7885  Dvi,
7886  /// An included figure exists only as PDF/PNG/JPEG, which latex+dvips cannot include: pdflatex.
7887  Pdf,
7888}
7889std::thread_local! {
7890  static FIGURE_OUTPUT_CUE: std::cell::Cell<FigureOutputCue> =
7891    const { std::cell::Cell::new(FigureOutputCue::Undecided) };
7892}

7894/// before the session's preloads load.
7895pub fn set_figure_output_cue(cue: FigureOutputCue) { FIGURE_OUTPUT_CUE.with(|c| c.set(cue)); }
7896/// The output-mode cue of the figures the document's source includes ([`set_figure_output_cue`]).
7897pub fn figure_output_cue() -> FigureOutputCue { FIGURE_OUTPUT_CUE.with(std::cell::Cell::get) }
7898std::thread_local! {
7899  static SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH: std::cell::Cell<Option<i64>> = const { std::cell::Cell::new(None) };
7900}
7901/// Records the date of the document's source (Unix seconds, UTC): the newest modification time among the TeX source
7902/// files of the bundle it came in (`Config::source_date_epoch`), `None` when the source carries none; set per
7903/// conversion before the session's preloads load. ar5iv's archival preload reads it as the job's clock.
7904pub fn set_source_date_epoch(epoch: Option<i64>) { SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH.with(|c| c.set(epoch)); }
7905/// The date of the document's source ([`set_source_date_epoch`]).

7908/// Snapshot of all registered (name, ext) binding pairs across all
7909/// dispatchers. Used by `find_file(notex=true)` to detect compiled-binding
7910/// existence regardless of extension (cls/sty/def/pool/code.tex/...).
7911pub fn get_binding_names() -> Vec<&'static [(&'static str, &'static str)]> {
7912  state!().binding_names.clone()
7913}
7914/// Append one crate's `(name, ext)` slice. Companion to
7915/// `set_bindings_dispatch` / `set_extra_bindings_dispatch` — call alongside
7916/// dispatcher registration so `find_file` can resolve compile-time
7917/// bindings. Duplicates are deduplicated by pointer so repeated calls from
7918/// the same crate don't inflate the fallback pool.
7919pub fn add_binding_names(names: &'static [(&'static str, &'static str)]) {
7920  let mut state = state_mut!();
7921  let ptr = names.as_ptr();
7922  if state.binding_names.iter().any(|s| s.as_ptr() == ptr) {
7923    return;
7924  }
7925  state.binding_names.push(names);
7926}
7927
7928/// Filtered view of `get_binding_names()` returning ONLY class names
7929/// (without `.cls` suffix). Used by `load_class` for Perl's prefix-match
7930/// fallback (Package.pm L2702-2706). Returns a flat `Vec<&str>` rather
7931/// than per-crate slices — callers that need to preserve crate boundaries
7932/// should iterate `get_binding_names()` directly.
7933pub fn get_class_binding_names() -> Vec<&'static str> {
7934  state!()
7935    .binding_names
7936    .iter()
7937    .flat_map(|slice| slice.iter())
7938    .filter(|(_, ext)| *ext == "cls")
7939    .map(|(name, _)| *name)
7940    .collect()
7941}
7942
7943/// `true` when at least one registered binding declares `ext` as its
7944/// extension. Used by `\input`'s heuristic to decide whether
7945/// `\input{name.<ext>}` should consult the binding registry — e.g.
7946/// `.sty`, `.cls`, `.def`, `.pool`, `code.tex` are all valid binding
7947/// extensions, while `.eps`, `.png`, `.bib` are not. Matches by extension
7948/// only (the `name` is checked separately by `dispatch()`'s exact lookup).
7949pub fn is_binding_extension(ext: &str) -> bool {
7950  state!()
7951    .binding_names
7952    .iter()
7953    .any(|slice| slice.iter().any(|(_, e)| *e == ext))
7954}
7955
7956/// `true` when a binding is registered for the exact `(name, ext)` pair.
7957/// Convenience wrapper over the per-crate slices in `binding_names`.
7958/// Mirrors `dispatch()`'s lookup but without the side effect of loading.
7959pub fn binding_exists(name: &str, ext: &str) -> bool {
7960  state!()
7961    .binding_names
7962    .iter()
7963    .any(|slice| slice.iter().any(|(n, e)| *n == name && *e == ext))
7964}

7967pub fn set_label_mapping_hook(hook: LabelMappingHook) {

7969  state.label_mapping_hook = Some(hook);

7972/// Read SEARCHPATHS from the group-scoped value table (Perl
7973/// `LookupValue('SEARCHPATHS')`). Mirrors [`get_graphics_paths`]: the list is a
7974/// group-scoped value, not a plain field, so an `\import`/`\subimport` group
7975/// reverts its change at `}` and a package's global add persists.
7976pub fn get_search_paths() -> Vec<String> {
7977  lookup_value("SEARCHPATHS")
7978    .map(|v| match v {
7979      Stored::Strings(syms) => syms.iter().map(|s| arena::to_string(*s)).collect(),
7980      Stored::VecDequeStored(vdq) => vdq
7981        .iter()
7982        .filter_map(|item| match item {
7983          Stored::String(s) => Some(arena::to_string(*s)),
7984          _ => None,
7985        })
7986        .collect(),
7987      _ => Vec::new(),
7988    })
7989    .unwrap_or_default()
7990}
7991pub fn with_search_paths<R, FnR>(caller: FnR) -> R
7992where FnR: FnOnce(&[String]) -> R {
7993  caller(&get_search_paths())
7994}
7995/// Global append (Perl `PushValue(SEARCHPATHS)`) — a persistent search dir.
7996pub fn add_search_path(path: String) {
7997  let mut paths = get_search_paths();
7998  paths.push(path);
7999  set_search_paths(paths);
8000}
8001/// Global prepend (Perl `UnshiftValue(SEARCHPATHS)`) — a persistent search dir.
8002pub fn search_paths_push_front(path: String) {
8003  let mut paths = get_search_paths();
8004  paths.insert(0, path);
8005  set_search_paths(paths);
8006}
8007/// Replace SEARCHPATHS GLOBALLY (Perl `AssignValue(SEARCHPATHS => [...], 'global')`).
8008/// For the local-by-default `\import` scoping, use [`set_search_paths_local`].
8009pub fn set_search_paths(paths: Vec<String>) { assign_search_paths(paths, Scope::Global); }
8010/// Replace SEARCHPATHS in the CURRENT group only (Perl `AssignValue(SEARCHPATHS
8011/// => [...])` default-local): reverted when the enclosing `\import`/`\subimport`
8012/// group closes. This is what makes `import.sty` faithful without an explicit
8013/// save/restore stack.
8014pub fn set_search_paths_local(paths: Vec<String>) { assign_search_paths(paths, Scope::Local); }
8015fn assign_search_paths(paths: Vec<String>, scope: Scope) {
8016  let vdq: VecDeque<Stored> = paths
8017    .into_iter()
8018    .map(|p| Stored::String(arena::pin(&p)))
8019    .collect();
8020  assign_value("SEARCHPATHS", Stored::VecDequeStored(vdq), Some(scope));
8021}
8022pub fn has_search_paths() -> bool { !get_search_paths().is_empty() }
8023/// Mirror Perl's `LookupValue('GRAPHICSPATHS')` — a list value that all
8024/// `\graphicspath`, `\svgpath`, initial source-directory prepends, and
8025/// `image_candidates` consult. Always return as `Vec<String>` even if the
8026/// value was stored as `Strings` (initial assignValue) or `VecDequeStored`
8027/// (after any push/unshift).
8028pub fn get_graphics_paths() -> Vec<String> {
8029  lookup_value("GRAPHICSPATHS")
8030    .map(|v| match v {
8031      Stored::Strings(syms) => syms.iter().map(|s| arena::to_string(*s)).collect(),
8032      Stored::VecDequeStored(vdq) => vdq
8033        .iter()
8034        .filter_map(|item| match item {
8035          Stored::String(s) => Some(arena::to_string(*s)),
8036          _ => None,
8037        })
8038        .collect(),
8039      _ => Vec::new(),
8040    })
8041    .unwrap_or_default()
8042}
8043
8044/// Zero-alloc membership test for GRAPHICSPATHS. Mirrors the Perl idiom
8045/// `grep { $_ eq $dir } @{ $state->lookupValue('GRAPHICSPATHS') }` but
8046/// without allocating an owned `Vec<String>` for a single boolean — the
8047/// interned-symbol `with`/`with2` family resolves each path in place.
8048pub fn graphics_paths_contains(needle: &str) -> bool {
8049  lookup_value("GRAPHICSPATHS")
8050    .map(|v| match v {
8051      Stored::Strings(syms) => syms.iter().any(|s| arena::with(*s, |p| p == needle)),
8052      Stored::VecDequeStored(vdq) => vdq.iter().any(|item| match item {
8053        Stored::String(s) => arena::with(*s, |p| p == needle),
8054        _ => false,
8055      }),
8056      _ => false,
8057    })
8058    .unwrap_or(false)
8059}
8060
8061/// Mirror Perl's `$state->unshiftValue(GRAPHICSPATHS => $dir)`. Used by
8062/// Core.pm-style source-directory prepends.
8063pub fn graphics_paths_push_front(path: String) {
8064  let key = arena::pin("GRAPHICSPATHS");
8065  let entry = Stored::String(arena::pin(&path));
8066  let mut state = state_mut!();
8067  state.journal_touch(TableName::Value, key);
8068  if !state.value.contains_key(&key) {
8069    state.assign_internal(
8070      TableName::Value,
8071      key,
8072      Stored::VecDequeStored(VecDeque::new()),
8073      Some(Scope::Global),
8074    );
8075  }
8076  let receiver = state.value.get_mut(&key).unwrap().front_mut();
8077  match receiver {
8078    Some(Stored::VecDequeStored(vdq)) => vdq.push_front(entry),
8079    Some(Stored::Strings(syms)) => {
8080      let mut vdq: VecDeque<Stored> = syms.iter().map(|s| Stored::String(*s)).collect();
8081      vdq.push_front(entry);
8082      state.assign_internal(
8083        TableName::Value,
8084        key,
8085        Stored::VecDequeStored(vdq),
8086        Some(Scope::Global),
8087      );
8088    },
8089    _ => {
8090      let mut vdq = VecDeque::new();
8091      vdq.push_front(entry);
8092      state.assign_internal(
8093        TableName::Value,
8094        key,
8095        Stored::VecDequeStored(vdq),
8096        Some(Scope::Global),
8097      );
8098    },
8099  }
8100}

8102/// manage a (global) hash of values
8103pub fn with_mapping<R, FnR>(map: &str, key: &str, caller: FnR) -> R
8104where FnR: FnOnce(Option<&Stored>) -> R {
8105  let map_sym = arena::pin(map);
8106  caller(match state!().value.get(&map_sym) {
8107    None => None,
8108    Some(map_vec) => match map_vec.front() {
8109      Some(Stored::HashStored(h)) => h.get(key),
8110      _ => None,
8111    },
8112  })
8113}

8115pub fn with_mapping_sym<R, FnR>(map: SymStr, key: SymStr, caller: FnR) -> R
8116where FnR: FnOnce(Option<&Stored>) -> R {
8117  caller(match state!().value.get(&map) {
8118    None => None,
8119    Some(map_vec) => match map_vec.front() {
8120      Some(Stored::HashStored(h)) => h.get_sym(key),
8121      _ => None,
8122    },
8123  })
8124}

8136pub fn get_input_encoding() -> Option<SymStr> { state!().input_encoding.as_ref().map(arena::pin) }
8137pub fn set_input_encoding(val: Option<String>) {
8138  let mut state = state_mut!();
8139  state.input_encoding = val;
8140}

8142pub fn with_stacked_values<R, FnR>(key: &str, caller: FnR) -> R
8143where FnR: FnOnce(Vec<&Stored>) -> R {
8144  caller(state!().lookup_stacked_values(key))
8145}
8146/// Sym-keyed variant of `with_stacked_values`.
8147pub fn with_stacked_values_sym<R, FnR>(key: SymStr, caller: FnR) -> R
8148where FnR: FnOnce(Vec<&Stored>) -> R {
8149  caller(state!().lookup_stacked_values_sym(key))
8150}

8152pub fn set_state(incoming_state: State) {
8153  // Reset state rotation to Main to prevent stale Sty/Std state from previous runs
8154  STATE_IN_USE.set(RotateState::Main);
8155  let mut global_state = state_mut!();
8156  *global_state = incoming_state;
8157}
8158
8159/// Check whether a Stored value can be serialized for the kernel dump.
8160/// Values containing closures (Primitive, Constructor, Conditional, etc.)
8161/// cannot be serialized — they come from Rust engine code, not the dump.
8162/// This matches Perl's DumpFile which only serializes Expandable macros.
8163pub fn is_serializable(stored: &Stored) -> bool {
8164  use Stored::*;
8165  match stored {
8166    // Data types: always serializable
8167    None | Bool(_) | String(_) | Charcode(_) | Int(_) | Catcode(_) => true,
8168    Token(_) | Tokens(_) | Number(_) | Float(_) => true,
8169    Glue(_) | MuGlue(_) | Dimension(_) | MuDimension(_) => true,
8170    Reversion(_) | KeyVal(_) => true,
8171    Chars(_) | Strings(_) => true,
8172    // Expandable: serializable when body is Tokens OR None (regular
8173    // macros). Closure-bodied Expandables (e.g. `\expandafter`,
8174    // `\unexpanded`, `\the` — defined via `DefMacro!` with a closure
8175    // body) ALSO pass — dump_writer's `serialize_stored` emits a PA
8176    // alias to the canonical CS so `\let \tex_expandafter:D
8177    // \expandafter`-style aliases survive the dump. (Bug C parity fix
8178    // — see project_kernel_dump_tdd.md.) The writer's add-only policy
8179    // at load time skips entries whose key is already defined in the
8180    // compiled engine, so primary CSes don't double-bind.
8181    Expandable(_) => true,
8182    // Register: serializable (stores value + type, no closures)
8183    Register(_) => true,
8184    // Font: serializable (data only)
8185    Font(_) => true,
8186    // A font's parameter array: serializable (data only), as an `IA` record.
8187    FontDimens(_) => true,
8188    // Primitives/MathPrimitives/Conditionals: the CLOSURE can't be
8189    // serialized, but each carries its own canonical CS name. If the
8190    // entry's key differs from that canonical CS, this is a `\let`-alias
8191    // we CAN capture (as a "PA" pointer) so the dump reader replays the
8192    // `\let` at load time. dump_writer returns the PA tag; dump_reader
8193    // re-applies via state::let_i. This is how \tex_let:D, \tex_def:D,
8194    // \tex_ifx:D, \if_meaning:w, and the hundreds of other expl3-renamed
8195    // primitives + conditionals survive the dump without needing to re-run
8196    // 36k lines of expl3-code.tex.
8197    //
8198    // Returning true here only means "pass to dump_writer"; the writer's
8199    // serialize_stored emits the PA target. Self-aliases (primary CSes
8200    // not yet aliased anywhere) typically don't appear in the diff because
8201    // they're in the pre-snapshot — but if they do, the dump reader skips
8202    // them by comparing key to target.
8203    Primitive(_) | MathPrimitive(_) | Conditional(_) => true,
8204    // Constructor: same logic as Primitive/Conditional. Constructors carry a
8205    // closure body the dump can't serialize, BUT they each carry a canonical
8206    // CS field. When the entry key differs from that CS, it's a `\let`-alias
8207    // (e.g. `\let \tex_par:D \par` where `\par` is itself a `Let!` alias to
8208    // `\lx@normal@par` — a Constructor). dump_writer emits `PA\t<cs>`;
8209    // dump_reader replays via `state::let_i`. Mirrors Perl's writer:
8210    // `dump_constructor` is undefined in `Dumper.pm`, but TeX_Job.pool
8211    // `DumpFile`'s let-detection branch (L184-198) catches the (key !=
8212    // value->getCSName) case and emits `Lt(key, letkey)`. Without this,
8213    // `\tex_par:D`, `\tex_cr:D`, `\tex_noindent:D`, etc. drop from the dump
8214    // because diff_from_snapshot filters them before the writer's
8215    // Constructor arm sees them.
8216    Constructor(_) => true,
8217    // Collections: serializable if contents are
8218    VecDequeStored(_) | HashStored(_) | HashString(_) => true,
8219    // Everything else: skip for safety
8220    _ => false,
8221  }
8222}
8223
8224/// Take a snapshot of the current State (for dump diff).
8225pub fn take_snapshot() -> rustc_hash::FxHashMap<(TableName, SymStr), Stored> { state!().snapshot() }
8226
8227/// Compute diff from snapshot and return changed serializable entries.
8228pub fn diff_snapshot(
8229  snap: &rustc_hash::FxHashMap<(TableName, SymStr), Stored>,
8230) -> Vec<(TableName, SymStr, Stored)> {
8231  state!().diff_from_snapshot(snap)
8232}
8233
8234// Thread-local holder for the snapshot taken at a named init phase.
8235// Currently only "bootstrap" is used: when `latex.rs` finishes loading
8236// `latex_bootstrap`, it stashes the state snapshot here. `ini_tex::dump_format`
8237// reads it so its diff matches Perl's `DumpFile` semantics — "what did raw
8238// latex.ltx + the rest of the engine init add on top of pure bootstrap".
8239// Without this hook the snapshot is taken after `_base.rs` + `_constructs.rs`
8240// have also run, making the diff far narrower than Perl's dump. See
8241// SYNC_STATUS D0 (d.1).
8242type StateSnapshot = rustc_hash::FxHashMap<(TableName, SymStr), Stored>;
8243type StagedSnapshotMap = rustc_hash::FxHashMap<&'static str, StateSnapshot>;
8244
8245thread_local! {
8246  static STAGED_SNAPSHOTS: RefCell<StagedSnapshotMap> =
8247    RefCell::new(rustc_hash::FxHashMap::default());
8248}
8249
8250/// Take a snapshot now and store it under a named key for later retrieval.
8251/// Intended for phased engine init (e.g. `stage_snapshot("bootstrap")` called
8252/// right after `latex_bootstrap` has loaded).
8253pub fn stage_snapshot(name: &'static str) {
8254  let snap = take_snapshot();
8255  STAGED_SNAPSHOTS.with(|m| {
8256    m.borrow_mut().insert(name, snap);
8257  });
8258}
8259
8260/// Stage an already-taken snapshot under a named key. Used by callers
8261/// (like `ini_tex`) that want to snapshot at a specific point without
8262/// waiting for a pool hook.
8263pub fn stage_snapshot_value(
8264  name: &'static str,
8265  snap: rustc_hash::FxHashMap<(TableName, SymStr), Stored>,
8266) {
8267  STAGED_SNAPSHOTS.with(|m| {
8268    m.borrow_mut().insert(name, snap);
8269  });
8270}
8271
8272/// Retrieve a previously staged snapshot, if present.
8273pub fn get_staged_snapshot(
8274  name: &str,
8275) -> Option<rustc_hash::FxHashMap<(TableName, SymStr), Stored>> {
8276  STAGED_SNAPSHOTS.with(|m| m.borrow().get(name).cloned())
8277}

8279#[cfg(test)]
8280mod conditional_counter_tests {
8281  use super::*;
8282
8283  /// Perl `Conditional.pm:61-62` assigns `if_count` *globally* so the
8284  /// per-conditional id keeps climbing across group exits; the typed field
8285  /// must not revert with the frame it was bumped in, and the limit is a
8286  /// plain global (`$LaTeXML::IF_LIMIT`), unlimited when `0`.
8287  #[test]
8288  fn if_ids_climb_across_group_exits() {
8289    reset_thread_state();
8290    let first = next_if_id();
8291    push_frame();
8292    let inner = next_if_id();
8293    pop_frame().expect("pop the probe frame");
8294    let after = next_if_id();
8295    assert_eq!((first, inner, after), (1, 2, 3));
8296    assert_eq!(
8297      if_limit(),
8298      0,
8299      "unlimited until latexml.sty's iflimit sets it"
8300    );
8301    set_if_limit(3_999_999);
8302    assert_eq!(if_limit(), 3_999_999);
8303    // The absorb counter is the same mechanism (Perl Object.pm:165).
8304    push_frame();
8305    assert_eq!(next_absorb_id(), 1);
8306    pop_frame().expect("pop the probe frame");
8307    assert_eq!(next_absorb_id(), 2);
8308    set_absorb_limit(7);
8309    assert_eq!(absorb_limit(), 7);
8310    reset_thread_state();
8311    assert_eq!(next_if_id(), 1, "a fresh conversion restarts the counter");
8312    assert_eq!(if_limit(), 0, "and forgets the previous limit");
8313    assert_eq!((next_absorb_id(), absorb_limit()), (1, 0));
8314  }
8315}

8317#[cfg(test)]
8318mod reentrancy_tests {
8319  use super::*;
8320
8321  /// A package-load hoist moves a meaning to global scope without changing whether it is a fallback class's
8322  /// guess (`fallback_meanings`), whatever origin is current while it runs.
8323  #[test]
8324  fn package_load_hoist_keeps_fallback_standing() {
8325    use crate::definition::origin::{DefinitionOrigin, with_origin};
8326    let guess = T_CS!("\\p1a_hoisted_fallback_probe");
8327    let own = T_CS!("\\p1a_hoisted_own_probe");
8328    push_frame();
8329    let pre = snapshot_top_frame_keys();
8330    with_origin(DefinitionOrigin::Fallback, || {
8331      let_i(&guess, &T_LETTER!("x"), None)
8332    });
8333    let_i(&own, &T_LETTER!("y"), None);
8334    with_origin(DefinitionOrigin::Fallback, || {
8335      hoist_top_frame_package_load(&pre)
8336    });
8337    assert!(pop_frame().is_ok());
8338    assert!(
8339      lookup_meaning(&guess).is_some() && lookup_meaning(&own).is_some(),
8340      "both hoisted to global scope"
8341    );
8342    assert!(is_fallback_meaning(&guess), "a fallback guess stays one");
8343    assert!(
8344      !is_fallback_meaning(&own),
8345      "a hoist under a fallback origin makes nothing a guess"
8346    );
8347  }
8348
8349  /// `with_let` binds for the body only and restores the prior meaning —
8350  /// including "no meaning" — even when the body fails.
8351  #[test]
8352  fn with_let_restores_the_prior_meaning() {
8353    let cs = T_CS!("\\p1a_with_let_probe");
8354    // A character token is its own meaning even in a bare State (no engine).
8355    let target = T_OTHER!("x");
8356    assert!(lookup_meaning(&cs).is_none());
8357    let seen: Result<bool> = with_let(&cs, &target, || Ok(lookup_meaning(&cs).is_some()));
8358    assert_eq!(seen.ok(), Some(true));
8359    assert!(lookup_meaning(&cs).is_none(), "restored to no meaning");
8360    let_i(&cs, &T_LETTER!("y"), None);
8361    let before = lookup_meaning(&cs);
8362    assert!(before.is_some());
8363    let failed: Result<()> = with_let(&cs, &target, || Err("boom".into()));
8364    assert!(failed.is_err());
8365    assert_eq!(lookup_meaning(&cs), before, "restored after a failing body");
8366  }
8367
8368  /// `try_lookup_int` must degrade to `None` under a live mutable borrow
8369  /// (contention) instead of panicking, while behaving like `lookup_int`
8370  /// otherwise. This is the load-bearing primitive of the `Error!`-during-
8371  /// `state_mut()` fix (tikz-cd 2001.08973).
8372  #[test]
8373  fn try_lookup_int_degrades_on_contention() {
8374    // Absent key, no contention → Some(0), matching lookup_int's default.
8375    assert_eq!(try_lookup_int("p1a_absent_key_xyz"), Some(0));
8376    // A live mutable borrow → None (cannot read), no panic.
8377    let _guard = (*STATE).borrow_mut();
8378    assert_eq!(try_lookup_int("MAX_ERRORS"), None);
8379  }
8380
8381  /// Reproduces tikz-cd 2001.08973: `push_value` into a non-VecDeque field
8382  /// hits the BUG-path `Error!`, which reads `MAX_ERRORS`. Before the fix,
8383  /// `push_value` held `state_mut!()` across that `Error!`, panicking
8384  /// "RefCell already mutably borrowed". It must now report the BUG and
8385  /// return Ok without panicking.
8386  #[test]
8387  fn push_value_bug_path_is_borrow_safe() {
8388    assign_value("p1a_bug_key", Stored::Int(7), Some(Scope::Global));
8389    let r = push_value("p1a_bug_key", Stored::Int(1));
8390    assert!(r.is_ok());
8391    // Same guarantee for the pop side.
8392    let r2 = pop_value("p1a_bug_key");
8393    assert!(r2.is_ok());
8394  }
8395
8396  /// `Scope::InPlace` (Perl `State.pm:175` 'inplace') is the same-level
8397  /// reassignment behind the Rhai `LookupDefinition(cs).push*` hook-splice
8398  /// (`install_definition(d, Some(Scope::InPlace))`). It must be neither Global
8399  /// nor Local across a group boundary — this is exactly the divergence
8400  /// @xworld21 flagged in PR #333 (r3623947537). Exercised on the Value table,
8401  /// which funnels through the identical `assign_internal` arm.
8402  #[test]
8403  fn inplace_scope_keeps_the_bindings_level() {
8404    // Scenario 1: a value bound ABOVE the group, mutated in-place from INSIDE
8405    // the group, PERSISTS past group exit (Local would have reverted it). This
8406    // is BookML's real case: patch an already-global def, mutation stays.
8407    assign_value("ip_above", Stored::Int(1), Some(Scope::Global));
8408    push_frame();
8409    assign_value("ip_above", Stored::Int(2), Some(Scope::InPlace));
8410    assert_eq!(
8411      lookup_int("ip_above"),
8412      2,
8413      "in-place mutation is active at once"
8414    );
8415    pop_frame().expect("pop group");
8416    assert_eq!(
8417      lookup_int("ip_above"),
8418      2,
8419      "in-place patch of an outer-bound value rode the outer binding past group \
8420       exit (Local would revert to 1)"
8421    );
8422
8423    // Scenario 2: a value LOCALLY redefined in the group, then mutated in-place,
8424    // REVERTS to the outer value at group exit (Global would have kept the
8425    // patch). The in-place edit rode the LOCAL binding, which is discarded.
8426    assign_value("ip_local", Stored::Int(1), Some(Scope::Global));
8427    push_frame();
8428    assign_value("ip_local", Stored::Int(2), Some(Scope::Local));
8429    assign_value("ip_local", Stored::Int(3), Some(Scope::InPlace));
8430    assert_eq!(
8431      lookup_int("ip_local"),
8432      3,
8433      "in-place mutated the local front"
8434    );
8435    pop_frame().expect("pop group");
8436    assert_eq!(
8437      lookup_int("ip_local"),
8438      1,
8439      "in-place patch of a locally-bound value was discarded with the group \
8440       (Global would keep 3)"
8441    );
8442  }
8443
8444  /// `is_scope_active` must track the FRONT value's truthiness, not key presence:
8445  /// `deactivate_scope` OVERWRITES the front value with `Stored::Bool(false)`
8446  /// instead of removing the entry, so a presence test reports a deactivated
8447  /// scope as still active.
8448  /// Perl writes the same test inline as `$$self{stash_active}{$scope}[0]`
8449  /// (State.pm L682). The region property `subfile_scope_at_depth` relies on
8450  /// (OXIDIZED_DESIGN #65) is the second assertion: an activation made inside a
8451  /// group is undone by that group, with no matching teardown call.
8452  #[test]
8453  fn scope_activity_tracks_value_not_presence() {
8454    let scope = arena::pin("t@scope@activity");
8455    assert!(!is_scope_active(scope), "unknown scope must be inactive");
8456
8457    activate_scope(scope);
8458    assert!(is_scope_active(scope), "activated scope must read active");
8459
8460    deactivate_scope(scope);
8461    assert!(
8462      !is_scope_active(scope),
8463      "a deactivated scope must read INACTIVE — `stash_active` still holds the \
8464       key, carrying Stored::Bool(false)"
8465    );
8466    // Deactivation is a GLOBAL assign, which replaces rather than layers: the
8467    // stack collapses to exactly that one falsy value. This is why the front
8468    // value is the whole state, and why presence cannot be the activity test.
8469    let depth = state!()
8470      .stash_active
8471      .get(&scope)
8472      .map(|entry| entry.len())
8473      .unwrap_or(0);
8474    assert_eq!(
8475      depth, 1,
8476      "global assign must overwrite, leaving one value — not stack a second"
8477    );
8478
8479    // A second deactivation must be a silent no-op. Perl gates on `[0]` being
8480    // TRUE (State.pm L700) precisely so the binding-pop below it does not run
8481    // twice; re-running it pops values `activate_scope` never pushed, which is
8482    // what Perl's "Unassigning wrong value for KEY from table T in
8483    // deactivateScope" warning reports.
8484    deactivate_scope(scope);
8485    assert!(
8486      !is_scope_active(scope),
8487      "still inactive after a second deactivate"
8488    );
8489    let depth2 = state!()
8490      .stash_active
8491      .get(&scope)
8492      .map(|entry| entry.len())
8493      .unwrap_or(0);
8494    assert_eq!(
8495      depth2, 1,
8496      "a second deactivation must not stack another value"
8497    );
8498  }
8499
8500  /// A DEACTIVATED scope must be activatable again — Perl gates `activateScope`
8501  /// on `!$$self{stash_active}{$scope}[0]` (State.pm L682), the front value's
8502  /// truthiness. Gating on key presence instead made the first deactivation
8503  /// permanent, since `deactivate_scope` leaves `Stored::Bool(false)` behind.
8504  /// Reached in Perl by the counter/label scopes, which deactivate the old
8505  /// reference number before activating the next (`Package.pm` L774-779).
8506  #[test]
8507  fn a_deactivated_scope_can_be_reactivated() {
8508    let scope = arena::pin("t@scope@reactivate");
8509    activate_scope(scope);
8510    deactivate_scope(scope);
8511    assert!(!is_scope_active(scope), "precondition: deactivated");
8512
8513    activate_scope(scope);
8514    assert!(
8515      is_scope_active(scope),
8516      "re-activation after deactivation must take effect (Perl State.pm L682)"
8517    );
8518  }
8519
8520  /// The self-terminating half: `activate_scope` marks `StashActive` with
8521  /// `Scope::Local`, so the enclosing group ends the region by construction.
8522  #[test]
8523  fn scope_activation_is_bounded_by_its_group() {
8524    let scope = arena::pin("t@scope@bounded");
8525    push_frame();
8526    activate_scope(scope);
8527    assert!(
8528      is_scope_active(scope),
8529      "active inside the group that opened it"
8530    );
8531    pop_frame().expect("pop group");
8532    assert!(
8533      !is_scope_active(scope),
8534      "the region must end with its group — no explicit deactivate_scope"
8535    );
8536  }
8537}

8539#[cfg(test)]
8540mod let_copy_tests {
8541  use super::*;
8542
8543  fn macro_of(cs: &str, body: &str) -> Stored {
8544    Stored::Expandable(Rc::new(Expandable {
8545      cs: T_CS!(cs),
8546      paramlist: None,
8547      expansion: Tokens::new(Explode!(body)).into(),
8548      ..Expandable::default()
8549    }))
8550  }
8551
8552  fn holds(cs: &str, def: &Stored) -> bool {
8553    matches!((lookup_meaning(&T_CS!(cs)), def),
8554      (Some(Stored::Expandable(a)), Stored::Expandable(b)) if Rc::ptr_eq(&a, b))
8555  }
8556
8557  fn body_of(cs: &str) -> String {
8558    match lookup_meaning(&T_CS!(cs)) {
8559      Some(Stored::Expandable(def)) => match def.get_expansion() {
8560        Some(ExpansionBody::Tokens(body)) => body.to_string(),
8561        None => String::new(),
8562        Some(ExpansionBody::Closure(_)) => "<closure>".to_string(),
8563      },
8564      other => format!("{other:?}"),
8565    }
8566  }
8567
8568  /// A `\let` copy still sharing the definition follows it to the late one — a copy a group
8569  /// shadows as well, once the group ends; a copy of an older definition keeps its own; the source
8570  /// itself is left to the caller; no copies, nothing moves. No clone of the placeholder is held,
8571  /// so its holders are exactly its bindings and the walk ends on the last copy found.
8572  #[test]
8573  fn let_copies_follow_a_late_definition() {
8574    reset_thread_state();
8575    let job = T_CS!("\\lcjob");
8576    let older = macro_of("\\lcjob", "older");
8577    install_definition(older.clone(), None);
8578    let_i(&T_CS!("\\lcstale"), &job, None);
8579    install_definition(macro_of("\\lcjob", ""), None);
8580    let_i(&T_CS!("\\lccopy"), &job, None);
8581    let_i(&T_CS!("\\lcshadowed"), &job, None);
8582    push_frame();
8583    let_i(&T_CS!("\\lcshadowed"), &T_CS!("\\lcstale"), None);
8584
8585    let name = macro_of("\\lcjob", "name");
8586    assert_eq!(rebind_let_copies(&job, &name), 2);
8587    assert!(holds("\\lccopy", &name));
8588    assert!(
8589      holds("\\lcshadowed", &older),
8590      "the group's own binding is untouched"
8591    );
8592    pop_frame().expect("pop the probe frame");
8593    assert!(
8594      holds("\\lcshadowed", &name),
8595      "the shadowed copy was re-pointed in place"
8596    );
8597    assert!(
8598      holds("\\lcstale", &older),
8599      "a copy of an older definition keeps it"
8600    );
8601    assert_eq!(
8602      body_of("\\lcjob"),
8603      "",
8604      "the source is the caller's to install"
8605    );
8606
8607    install_definition(macro_of("\\lcsolo", "x"), None);
8608    assert_eq!(
8609      rebind_let_copies(&T_CS!("\\lcsolo"), &macro_of("\\lcsolo", "y")),
8610      0
8611    );
8612  }
8613}

8615#[cfg(test)]
8616mod assignment_journal_tests {
8617  use super::*;
8618
8619  /// A rollback puts back a meaning's standing as a fallback guess with the meaning (`fallback_meanings`): a group-local
8620  /// `\let` of it under the journal cleared the standing, which the group's end does not put back (FM S2a.2 r9: the
8621  /// observer rehearsal's `\let\AND`, OmniBus's `\AND` under the siamart binding, 2409.10200); and a name the journal
8622  /// saw become a guess is none after it.
8623  #[test]
8624  fn rollback_restores_a_fallback_standing() {
8625    use crate::definition::origin::{DefinitionOrigin, with_origin};
8626    let guess = T_CS!("\\r9_fallback_standing_probe");
8627    let fresh = T_CS!("\\r9_fallback_fresh_probe");
8628    with_origin(DefinitionOrigin::Fallback, || {
8629      let_i(&guess, &T_LETTER!("x"), Some(Scope::Global))
8630    });
8631    assert!(is_fallback_meaning(&guess));
8632    let journal = begin_assignment_journal().expect("no journal open");
8633    push_frame();
8634    let_i(&guess, &T_LETTER!("y"), None);
8635    with_origin(DefinitionOrigin::Fallback, || {
8636      let_i(&fresh, &T_LETTER!("z"), Some(Scope::Global))
8637    });
8638    assert!(pop_frame().is_ok());
8639    assert!(
8640      !is_fallback_meaning(&guess),
8641      "the group's end leaves the standing cleared"
8642    );
8643    assert!(journal.rollback(), "the frames it began with are open");
8644    assert!(is_fallback_meaning(&guess), "the rollback puts it back");
8645    assert!(!is_fallback_meaning(&fresh) && lookup_meaning(&fresh).is_none());
8646  }
8647
8648  /// A rollback puts back a global and a local assignment made since the journal began; a kept journal keeps them.
8649  #[test]
8650  fn rollback_undoes_and_keep_keeps() {
8651    assign_value("j65e_probe", "before", Some(Scope::Global));
8652    let journal = begin_assignment_journal().expect("no journal open");
8653    push_frame();
8654    assign_value("j65e_probe", "inner", None);
8655    assign_value("j65e_probe", "global", Some(Scope::Global));
8656    assert!(pop_frame().is_ok());
8657    assert_eq!(lookup_string("j65e_probe"), "global");
8658    assert!(journal.rollback(), "the frames it began with are open");
8659    assert_eq!(lookup_string("j65e_probe"), "before");
8660    let journal = begin_assignment_journal().expect("no journal open");
8661    assign_value("j65e_probe", "kept", Some(Scope::Global));
8662    journal.keep();
8663    assert_eq!(lookup_string("j65e_probe"), "kept");
8664  }
8665
8666  /// A journal that began inside a frame closed since cannot roll back: the assignments stay.
8667  #[test]
8668  fn rollback_refused_when_its_frames_changed() {
8669    push_frame();
8670    let journal = begin_assignment_journal().expect("no journal open");
8671    assign_value("j65e_frames", "set", Some(Scope::Global));
8672    assert!(pop_frame().is_ok());
8673    assert!(!journal.rollback(), "a frame it began with is gone");
8674    assert_eq!(lookup_string("j65e_frames"), "set");
8675  }
8676
8677  /// A guard dropped while the state is borrowed leaves its journal stale: nothing records into it, and the next begin
8678  /// discards it.
8679  #[test]
8680  fn stale_journal_records_nothing_and_is_discarded() {
8681    let journal = begin_assignment_journal().expect("no journal open");
8682    {
8683      let _held = STATE.borrow_mut();
8684      drop(journal);
8685    }
8686    assert!(state!().journal.is_some(), "the guard could not close it");
8687    assign_value("j65e_stale", "x", Some(Scope::Global));
8688    assert!(
8689      state!()
8690        .journal
8691        .as_ref()
8692        .is_some_and(|j| j.saved.is_empty()),
8693      "a stale journal records nothing"
8694    );
8695    let journal = begin_assignment_journal().expect("the stale journal is discarded");
8696    journal.keep();
8697    assert!(state!().journal.is_none());
8698  }
8699}
